Uninitialized = 0,\n21.     /// The `OnceCell` is being initialized.\n22.     Initializing = 1,\n23.     /// The `OnceCell` is initialized.\n24.     Initialized = 2,\n25. }\n26. \n27. impl From<usize> for State {\n28.     fn from(val: usize) -> Self {\n29.         match val {\n30.             0 => State::Uninitialized,\n31.             1 => State::Initializing,\n32.             2 => State::Initialized,\n33.             _ => unreachable!(\"Invalid state\"),\n34.         }\n35.     }\n36. }\n37. \n38. impl From<State> for usize {\n39.     fn from(val: State) -> Self {\n40.         val as usize\n41.     }\n42. }\n43. \n44. /// A memory location that can be written to at most once.\n45. ///\n46. /// A `OnceCell` can be used to store a single value, and only once. However,\n47. /// once the value is stored, it can be accessed directly through a reference\n48. /// instead of needing an RAII guard like `Mutex` or `RwLock`.\n49. ///\n50. /// # Examples\n51. ///\n52. /// This structure is useful for a variety of patterns, most notably for one-time\n53. /// initialization.\n54. ///\n55. /// ```rust\n56. /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n57. ///\n58. /// # struct Foobar;\n59. ///\n60. /// async fn very_expensive_initialization() -> Foobar {\n61. ///     // Imagine this is very expensive to initialize,\n62. ///     // for instance, it requires a network request or\n63. ///     // a database call.\n64. ///     # Foobar\n65. /// }\n66. ///\n67. /// struct LazyFoobar {\n68. ///     inner: OnceCell<Foobar>,\n69. /// }\n70. ///\n71. /// impl LazyFoobar {\n72. ///     fn new() -> Self {\n73. ///         Self {\n74. ///             inner: OnceCell::new(),\n75. ///         }\n76. ///     }\n77. ///\n78. ///     async fn load(&self) -> &Foobar {\n79. ///         self.inner.get_or_init(|| async {\n80. ///             very_expensive_initialization().await\n81. ///         }).await\n82. ///     }\n83. /// }\n84. /// ```\n85. pub struct OnceCell<T> {\n86.     /// Listeners waiting for a chance to initialize the cell.\n87.     ///\n88.     /// These are the users of get_or_init() and similar functions.\n89.     active_initializers: Event,\n90. \n91.     /// Listeners waiting for the cell to be initialized.\n92.     ///\n93.     /// These are the users of wait().\n94.     passive_waiters: Event,\n95. \n96.     /// State associated with the cell.\n97.     state: AtomicUsize,\n98. \n99.     /// The value of the cell.\n100.     value: UnsafeCell<MaybeUninit<T>>,\n101. }\n102. \n103. unsafe impl<T: Send> Send for OnceCell<T> {}\n104. unsafe impl<T: Send + Sync> Sync for OnceCell<T> {}\n105. \n106. impl<T> OnceCell<T> {\n107.     /// Create a new, uninitialized `OnceCell`.\n108.     ///\n109.     /// # Example\n110.     ///\n111.     /// ```rust\n112.     /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n113.     ///\n114.     /// let cell = OnceCell::new();\n115.     /// # cell.set_blocking(1);\n116.     /// ```\n117.     pub const fn new() -> Self {\n118.         Self {\n119.             active_initializers: Event::new(),\n120.             passive_waiters: Event::new(),\n121.             state: AtomicUsize::new(State::Uninitialized as _),\n122.             value: UnsafeCell::new(MaybeUninit::uninit()),\n123.         }\n124.     }\n125. \n126.     /// Tell whether or not the cell is initialized.\n127.     ///\n128.     /// This may not always be accurate. For instance, it is possible for\n129.     /// another thread to initialize the cell between the time when this\n130.     /// function is called and the time when the result is actually used.\n131.     ///\n132.     /// # Example\n133.     ///\n134.     /// ```rust\n135.     /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n136.     ///\n137.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n138.     /// let cell = OnceCell::new();\n139.     /// assert!(!cell.is_initialized());\n140.     /// cell.set(1).await;\n141.     /// assert!(cell.is_initialized());\n142.     /// # });\n143.     /// ```\n144.     pub fn is_initialized(&self) -> bool {\n145.         State::from(self.state.load(Ordering::Acquire)) == State::Initialized\n146.     }\n147. \n148.     /// Get a reference to the inner value, or `None` if the value\n149.     /// is not yet initialized.\n150.     ///\n151.     /// # Example\n152.     ///\n153.     /// ```rust\n154.     /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n155.     ///\n156.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n157.     /// let cell = OnceCell::new();\n158.     /// assert!(cell.get().is_none());\n159.     /// cell.set(1).await;\n160.     /// assert_eq!(cell.get(), Some(&1));\n161.     /// # });\n162.     /// ```\n163.     pub fn get(&self) -> Option<&T> {\n164.         if self.is_initialized() {\n165.             // SAFETY: We know that the value is initialized, so it is safe to\n166.             // read it.\n167.             Some(unsafe { self.get_unchecked() })\n168.         } else {\n169.             None\n170.         }\n171.     }\n172. \n173.     /// Get a mutable reference to the inner value, or `None` if the value\n174.     /// is not yet initialized.\n175.     ///\n176.     /// This function is useful for initializing the value inside the cell\n177.     /// when we still have a mutable reference to the cell.\n178.     ///\n179.     /// # Example\n180.     ///\n181.     /// ```rust\n182.     /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n183.     ///\n184.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n185.     /// let mut cell = OnceCell::new();\n186.     /// assert!(cell.get_mut().is_none());\n187.     /// cell.set(1).await;\n188.     /// assert_eq!(cell.get_mut(), Some(&mut 1));\n189.     /// *cell.get_mut().unwrap() = 2;\n190.     /// assert_eq!(cell.get(), Some(&2));\n191.     /// # });\n192.     /// ```\n193.     pub fn get_mut(&mut self) -> Option<&mut T> {\n194.         if State::from(*self.state.get_mut()) == State::Initialized {\n195.             // SAFETY: We know that the value is initialized, so it is safe to\n196.             // read it.\n197.             Some(unsafe { &mut *self.value.get().cast() })\n198.         } else {\n199.             None\n200.         }\n201.     }\n202. \n203.     /// Take the value out of this `OnceCell`, moving it back to the uninitialized\n204.     /// state.\n205.     ///\n206.     /// # Example\n207.     ///\n208.     /// ```rust\n209.     /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n210.     ///\n211.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n212.     /// let mut cell = OnceCell::new();\n213.     /// cell.set(1).await;\n214.     /// assert_eq!(cell.take(), Some(1));\n215.     /// assert!(!cell.is_initialized());\n216.     /// # });\n217.     /// ```\n218.     pub fn take(&mut self) -> Option<T> {\n219.         if State::from(*self.state.get_mut()) == State::Initialized {\n220.             // SAFETY: We know that the value is initialized, so it is safe to\n221.             // read it.\n222.             let value = unsafe { ptr::read(self.value.get().cast()) };\n223.             *self.state.get_mut() = State::Uninitialized.into();\n224.             Some(value)\n225.         } else {\n226.             None\n227.         }\n228.     }\n229. \n230.     /// Convert this `OnceCell` into the inner value, if it is initialized.\n231.     ///\n232.     /// # Example\n233.     ///\n234.     /// ```rust\n235.     /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n236.     ///\n237.     /// # });\n375.     /// ```\n376.     pub async fn get_or_try_init<E, Fut: Future<Output = Result<T, E>>>(\n377.         &self,\n378.         closure: impl FnOnce() -> Fut,\n379.     ) -> Result<&T, E> {\n380.         // Fast path: see if the value is already initialized.\n381.         if let Some(value) = self.get() {\n382.             return Ok(value);\n383.         }\n384. \n385.         // Slow path: initialize the value.\n386.         self.initialize_or_wait(closure, &mut NonBlocking::default())\n387.             .await?;\n388.         debug_assert!(self.is_initialized());\n389. \n390.         // SAFETY: We know that the value is initialized, so it is safe to\n391.         // read it.\n392. Err(infallible) => match infallible {},\n478.         }\n479.     }\n480. \n481.     /// Either get the value or initialize it with the given closure.\n482.     ///\n483.     /// Many tasks may call this function, but the value will only be set once\n484.     /// and only one closure will be invoked.\n485.     ///\n486.     /// # Blocking\n487.     ///\n488.     /// In contrast to the `get_or_init` method, this method blocks the current thread of\n489.     /// execution instead of awaiting.\n490.     ///\n491.     /// This method should not be used in an asynchronous context. Ok(value) => value,\n511.             Err(infallible) => match infallible {},\n512.         }\n513.     }\n514. \n515.     /// Try to set the value of the cell.\n516.     ///\n517.     /// If the cell is already initialized, this method returns the original\n518.     /// value back.\n519.     ///\n520.     /// # Example\n521.     ///\n522.     /// ```rust\n523.     /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n524.     ///\n525.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n526.     /// let cell = OnceCell::new();\n527.     ///\n528.     /// assert_eq!(cell.set(1).await, Ok(&1));\n529.     /// assert_eq!(cell.get(), Some(&1));\n530.     /// assert_eq!(cell.set(2).await, Err(2));\n531.     /// # });\n532.     /// ```\n533.     pub async fn set(&self, value: T) -> Result<&T, T> {\n534.         let mut value = Some(value);\n535.         self.get_or_init(|| async { value.take().unwrap() }).await;\n536. \n537.         match value {\n538. However,\n /// once the value is stored, it can be accessed directly through a reference\n /// instead of needing an RAII guard like `Mutex` or `RwLock`.\n ///\n /// # Examples\n ///\n /// This structure is useful for a variety of patterns, most notably for one-time\n /// initialization.\n ///\n /// ```rust\n /// use async_lock::OnceCell;\n ///\n /// # Barrier {\n42.             n,\n43.             state: Mutex::new(State {\n44.                 count: 0,\n45.                 generation_id: 0,\n46.             }),\n47.             event: Event::new(),\n48.         }\n49.     }\n50. \n51.     /// Blocks the current task until all tasks reach this point.\n52.     ///\n53.     /// Barriers are reusable after all tasks have synchronized, and can be used continuously.\n54.     ///\n55.     /// Returns a [`BarrierWaitResult`] indicating whether this task is the \"leader\", meaning the\n56.     /// last task to call this method.\n57.     ///\n58.     /// # Examples\n59.     ///\n60.     /// ```\n61.     /// use async_lock::Barrier;\n62.     /// use futures_lite::future;\n63.     /// use std::sync::Arc;\n64.     /// use std::thread;\n65.     ///\n66.     /// let barrier = Arc::new(Barrier::new(5));\n67.     ///\n68.     /// for _ in 0..5 {\n69.     ///     let b = barrier.clone();\n70.     ///     thread::spawn(move || {\n71.     ///         future::block_on(async {\n72.     ///             // The same messages will be printed together.\n73.     ///             // There will NOT be interleaving of \"before\" and \"after\".\n74.     ///             println!(\"before wait\");\n75.     ///             b.wait().await;\n76.     ///             println!(\"after wait\");\n77.     ///         });\n78.     ///     });\n79.     /// }\n80.     /// ```\n81.     pub fn wait(&self) -> BarrierWait<'_> {\n82.         BarrierWait::_new(BarrierWaitInner {\n83.             barrier: self,\n84.             lock: Some(self.state.lock()),\n85.             evl: EventListener::new(&self.event),\n86.             state: WaitState::Initial,\n87.         })\n88.     }\n89. \n90.     /// Blocks the current thread until all tasks reach this point.\n91.     ///\n92.     /// Barriers are reusable after all tasks have synchronized, and can be used continuously.\n93.     ///\n94.     /// Returns a [`BarrierWaitResult`] indicating whether this task is the \"leader\", meaning the\n95.     /// last task to call this method.\n96.     ///\n97.     /// # Blocking\n98.     ///\n99.     /// Rather than using asynchronous waiting, like the [`wait`][`Barrier::wait`] method,\n100.     /// this method will block the current thread until the wait is complete.\n101.     ///\n102.     /// This method should not be used in an asynchronous context. It is intended to be\n103.     /// used in a way that a barrier can be used in both asynchronous and synchronous contexts.\n104.     /// Calling this method in an asynchronous context may result in a deadlock.\n105.     ///\n106.     /// # Examples\n107.     ///\n108.     /// ```\n109.     /// use async_lock::Barrier;\n110.     /// use futures_lite::future;\n111.     /// use std::sync::Arc;\n112.     /// use std::thread;\n113.     ///\n114.     /// let barrier = Arc::new(Barrier::new(5));\n115.     ///\n116.     /// for _ in 0..5 {\n117.     ///     let b = barrier.clone();\n118.     ///     thread::spawn(move || {\n119.     ///         // The same messages will be printed together.\n120.     ///         // There will NOT be interleaving of \"before\" and \"after\".\n121.     ///         println!(\"before wait\");\n122.     ///         b.wait_blocking();\n123.     ///         println!(\"after wait\");\n124.     ///     });\n125.     /// }\n126.     /// ```\n127.     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n128.     pub fn wait_blocking(&self) -> BarrierWaitResult {\n129.         self.wait().wait()\n130.     }\n131. }\n132. \n133. easy_wrapper! {\n134.     /// The future returned by [`Barrier::wait()`].\n135.     pub struct BarrierWait<'a>(BarrierWaitInner<'a> => BarrierWaitResult);\n136.     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n137.     pub(crate) wait();\n138. }\n139. \n140. pin_project_lite::pin_project! {\n141.     /// The future returned by [`Barrier::wait()`].\n142.     struct BarrierWaitInner<'a> {\n143.         // The barrier to wait on.\n144.         barrier: &'a Barrier,\n145. \n146.         // The ongoing mutex lock operation we are blocking on.\n147.         #[pin]\n148.         lock: Option<Lock<'a, State>>,\n149. \n150.         // An event listener for the `barrier.event` event.\n151.         #[pin]\n152.         evl: EventListener,\n153. \n154.         // The current state of the future.\n155.         state: WaitState,\n156.     }\n157. }\n158. \n159. impl fmt::Debug for BarrierWait<'_> {\n160.     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n161.         f.write_str(\"BarrierWait { .. }\")\n162.     }\n163. }\n164. \n165. enum WaitState {\n166.     /// We are getting the original values of the state.\n167.     Initial,\n168. \n169.     /// We are waiting for the listener to complete.\n170.     Waiting { local_gen: u64 },\n171. \n172.     /// Waiting to re-acquire the lock to check the state again.\n173.     Reacquiring { local_gen: u64 },\n174. }\n175. \n176. impl EventListenerFuture for BarrierWaitInner<'_> {\n177.     type Output = BarrierWaitResult;\n178. \n179.     fn poll_with_strategy<'a, S: Strategy<'a>>(\n180.         self: Pin<&mut Self>,\n181.         strategy: &mut S,\n182.         cx: &mut S::Context,\n183.     ) -> Poll<Self::Output> {\n184.         let mut this = self.project();\n185. \n186.         loop {\n187.             match this.state {\n188.                 WaitState::Initial => {\n189.                     // See if the lock is ready yet.\n190.                     let mut state = ready!(this\n191.                         .lock\n192.                         .as_mut()\n193.                         .as_pin_mut()\n194.                         .unwrap()\n195.                         .poll_with_strategy(strategy, cx));\n196.                     this.lock.as_mut().set(None);\n197. \n198.                     let local_gen = state.generation_id;\n199.                     state.count += 1;\n200. \n201.                     if state.count < this.barrier.n {\n202.                         // We need to wait for the event.\n203.                         this.evl.as_mut().listen();\n204.                         *this.state = WaitState::Waiting { local_gen };\n205.                     } else {\n206.                         // We are the last one.\n207.                         state.count = 0;\n208.                         state.generation_id = state.generation_id.wrapping_add(1);\n209.                         this.barrier.event.notify(core::usize::MAX);\n210.                         return Poll::Ready(BarrierWaitResult { is_leader: true });\n211.                     }\n212.                 }\n213. \n214.                 WaitState::Waiting { local_gen } => {\n215.                     ready!(strategy.poll(this.evl.as_mut(), cx));\n216. \n217.                     // We are now re-acquiring the mutex.\n218.                     this.lock.as_mut().set(Some(this.barrier.state.lock()));\n219.                     *this.state = WaitState::Reacquiring {\n220.                         local_gen: *local_gen,\n221.                     };\n222.                 }\n223. \n224.                 WaitState::Reacquiring { local_gen } => {\n225.                     // Acquire the local state again.\n226.                     let state = ready!(this\n227.                         .lock\n228.                         .as_mut()\n229.                         .as_pin_mut()\n230.                         .unwrap()\n231.                         .poll_with_strategy(strategy, cx));\n232.                     this.lock.set(None);\n233. \n234.                     if *local_gen == state.generation_id && state.count < this.barrier.n {\n235.                         // We need to wait for the event again.\n236.                         this.evl.as_mut().listen();\n237.                         *this.state = WaitState::Waiting {\n238.                             local_gen: *local_gen,\n239.                         };\n240.                     } else {\n241.                         // We are ready, but not the leader.\n242.                         return Poll::Ready(BarrierWaitResult { is_leader: false });\n243.                     }\n244.                 }\n245.             }\n246.         }\n247.     }\n248. }\n249. \n250. /// Returned by [`Barrier::wait()`] when all tasks have called it.\n251. ///\n252. /// # Examples\n253. ///\n254. /// ```\n255. /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n256. /// use async_lock::Barrier;\n257. ///\n258. /// let barrier = Barrier::new(1);\n259. /// let barrier_wait_result = barrier.wait().await;\n260. /// # });\n261. /// ```\n262. #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n263. pub struct BarrierWaitResult {\n264.     is_leader: bool,\n265. }\n266. \n267. impl BarrierWaitResult {\n268.     /// Returns `true` if this task was the last to call to [`Barrier::wait()`].\n269.     ///\n270.     /// # Mutex {\n70.             state: AtomicUsize::new(0),\n71.             lock_ops: Event::new(),\n72.             data: UnsafeCell::new(data),\n73.         }\n74.     }\n75. \n76.     /// Consumes the mutex, returning the underlying data.\n77.     ///\n78.     /// # Examples\n79.     ///\n80.     /// ```\n81.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n82.     ///\n83.     /// let mutex = Mutex::new(10);\n84.     /// assert_eq!(mutex.into_inner(), 10);\n85.     /// ```\n86.     pub fn into_inner(self) -> T {\n87.         self.data.into_inner()\n88.     }\n89. }\n90. \n91. impl<T: ?Sized> Mutex<T> {\n92.     /// Acquires the mutex.\n93.     ///\n94.     /// Returns a guard that releases the mutex when dropped.\n95.     ///\n96.     /// # Examples\n97.     ///\n98.     /// ```\n99.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n100.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n101.     ///\n102.     /// let mutex = Mutex::new(10);\n103.     /// let guard = mutex.lock().await;\n104.     /// assert_eq!(*guard, 10);\n105.     /// # })\n106.     /// ```\n107.     #[inline]\n108.     pub fn lock(&self) -> Lock<'_, T> {\n109. It is intended to be\n125.     /// used in a way that a mutex can be used in both asynchronous and synchronous contexts.\n126.     /// Calling this method in an asynchronous context may result in a deadlock.\n127.     ///\n128.     /// # Examples\n129.     ///\n130.     /// ```\n131.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n132.     ///\n133.     /// let mutex = Mutex::new(10);\n134.     /// let guard = mutex.lock_blocking();\n135.     /// assert_eq!(*guard, 10);\n136.     /// ```\n137.     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n138.     #[inline]\n139.     pub fn lock_blocking(&self) -> MutexGuard<'_, T> {\n140.         self.lock().wait()\n141.     }\n142. \n143.     /// Attempts to acquire the mutex.\n144.     ///\n145.     /// If the mutex could not be acquired at this time, then [`None`] is returned. Otherwise, a\n146.     /// guard is returned that releases the mutex when dropped.\n147.     ///\n148.     /// # Examples\n149.     ///\n150.     /// ```\n151.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n152.     ///\n153.     /// let mutex = Mutex::new(10);\n154.     /// if let Some(guard) = mutex.try_lock() {\n155.     ///     assert_eq!(*guard, 10);\n156.     /// }\n157.     /// # ;\n158.     /// ```\n159.     #[inline]\n160.     pub fn try_lock(&self) -> Option<MutexGuard<'_, T>> {\n161.         if self\n162.             .state\n163.             .compare_exchange(0, 1, Ordering::Acquire, Ordering::Acquire)\n164.             .is_ok()\n165.         {\n166.             Some(MutexGuard(self))\n167.         } else {\n168.             None\n169.         }\n170.     }\n171. \n172.     /// Returns a mutable reference to the underlying data.\n173.     ///\n174.     /// Since this call borrows the mutex mutably, no actual locking takes place -- the mutable\n175.     /// borrow statically guarantees the mutex is not already acquired.\n176.     ///\n177.     /// # Examples\n178.     ///\n179.     /// ```\n180.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n181.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n182.     ///\n183.     /// let mut mutex = Mutex::new(0);\n184.     /// *mutex.get_mut() = 10;\n185.     /// assert_eq!(*mutex.lock().await, 10);\n186.     /// # })\n187.     /// ```\n188.     pub fn get_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {\n189.         unsafe { &mut *self.data.get() }\n190.     }\n191. \n192.     /// Unlocks the mutex directly.\n193.     ///\n194.     /// # Safety\n195.     ///\n196.     /// This function is intended to be used only in the case where the mutex is locked,\n197.     /// and the guard is subsequently forgotten. Calling this while you don't hold a lock\n198.     /// on the mutex will likely lead to UB.\n199.     pub(crate) unsafe fn unlock_unchecked(&self) {\n200.         // Remove the last bit and notify a waiting lock operation.\n201.         self.state.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Release);\n202.         self.lock_ops.notify(1);\n203.     }\n204. }\n205. \n206. impl<T: ?Sized> Mutex<T> {\n207.     /// Acquires the mutex and clones a reference to it.\n208.     ///\n209.     /// Returns an owned guard that releases the mutex when dropped.\n210.     ///\n211.     /// # Examples\n212.     ///\n213.     /// ```\n214.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n215.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n216.     /// use std::sync::Arc;\n217.     ///\n218.     /// let mutex = Arc::new(Mutex::new(10));\n219.     /// let guard = mutex.lock_arc().await;\n220.     /// assert_eq!(*guard, 10);\n221.     /// # })\n222.     /// ```\n223.     #[inline]\n224.     pub fn lock_arc(self: &Arc<Self>) -> LockArc<T> {\n225.         LockArc::_new(LockArcInnards::Unpolled {\n226.             mutex: Some(self.clone()),\n227.         })\n228.     }\n229. \n230.     /// Acquires the mutex and clones a reference to it using the blocking strategy.\n231.     ///\n232.     /// Returns an owned guard that releases the mutex when dropped.\n233.     ///\n234.     /// # Blocking\n235.     ///\n236.     /// Rather than using asynchronous waiting, like the [`lock_arc`][Mutex::lock_arc] method,\n237.     /// this method will block the current thread until the lock is acquired.\n238.     ///\n239.     /// This method should not be used in an asynchronous context. It is intended to be\n240.     /// used in a way that a mutex can be used in both asynchronous and synchronous contexts.\n241.     /// Calling this method in an asynchronous context may result in a deadlock.\n242.     ///\n243.     /// # Examples\n244.     ///\n245.     /// ```\n246.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n247.     /// use std::sync::Arc;\n248.     ///\n249.     /// let mutex = Arc::new(Mutex::new(10));\n250.     /// let guard = mutex.lock_arc_blocking();\n251.     /// assert_eq!(*guard, 10);\n252.     /// ```\n253.     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n254.     #[inline]\n255.     pub fn lock_arc_blocking(self: &Arc<Self>) -> MutexGuardArc<T> {\n256.         self.lock_arc().wait()\n257.     }\n258. \n259.     /// Attempts to acquire the mutex and clone a reference to it.\n260.     ///\n261.     /// If the mutex could not be acquired at this time, then [`None`] is returned. Otherwise, an\n262.     /// owned guard is returned that releases the mutex when dropped.\n263.     ///\n264.     /// # Examples\n265.     ///\n266.     /// ```\n267.     /// use async_lock::Mutex;\n268.     /// use std::sync::Arc;\n269.     ///\n270.     /// let mutex = Arc::new(Mutex::new(10));\n271.     /// if let Some(guard) = mutex.try_lock() {\n272.     ///     assert_eq!(*guard, 10);\n273.     /// }\n274.     /// # ;\n275.     /// ```\n276.     #[inline]\n277.     pub fn try_lock_arc(self: &Arc<Self>) -> Option<MutexGuardArc<T>> {\n278.         if self\n279.             .state\n280.             .compare_exchange(0, 1, Ordering::Acquire, Ordering::Acquire)\n281.             .is_ok()\n282.         {\n283.             Some(MutexGuardArc(self.clone()))\n284.         } else {\n285.             None\n286.         }\n287.     }\n288. }\n289. \n290. impl<T: fmt::Debug + ?Sized> fmt::Debug for Mutex<T> {\n291.     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n292.         struct Locked;\n293.         impl fmt::Debug for Locked {\n294.             fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n295.                 f.write_str(\"<locked>\")\n296.             }\n297.         }\n298. \n299.         match self.try_lock() {\n300.             None => f.debug_struct(\"Mutex\").field(\"data\", &Locked).finish(),\n301.             Some(guard) => f.debug_struct(\"Mutex\").field(\"data\", &&*guard).finish(),\n302.         }\n303.     }\n304. }\n305. \n306. impl<T> From<T> for Mutex<T> {\n307.     fn from(val: T) -> Mutex<T> {\n308.         Mutex::new(val)\n309.     }\n310. }\n311. \n312. impl<T: Default + ?Sized> Default for Mutex<T> {\n313.     fn default() -> Mutex<T> {\n314.         Mutex::new(Default::default())\n315.     }\n316. }\n317. \n318. easy_wrapper! {\n319.     /// The future returned by [`Mutex::lock`].\n320.     pub struct Lock<'a, T: ?Sized>(LockInner<'a, T> => MutexGuard<'a, T>);\n321.     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n322.     pub(crate) wait();\n323. }\n324. \n325. pin_project_lite::pin_project! {\n326.     /// Inner future for acquiring the mutex.\n327.     struct LockInner<'a, T: ?Sized> {\n328.         // Reference to the mutex.\n329.         mutex: &'a Mutex<T>,\n330. \n331.         // The future that waits for the mutex to become available.\n332.         #[pin]\n333.         acquire_slow: Option<AcquireSlow<&'a Mutex<T>, T>>,\n334.     }\n335. }\n336. \n337. unsafe impl<T: Send + ?Sized> Send for Lock<'_, T> {}\n338. unsafe impl<T: Sync + ?Sized> Sync for Lock<'_, T> {}\n339. \n340. impl<T: ?Sized> fmt::Debug for LockInner<'_, T> {\n341.     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n342.         f.write_str(\"Lock { .. }\")\n343.     }\n344. }\n345. \n346. impl<'a, T: ?Sized> EventListenerFuture for LockInner<'a, T> {\n347.     type Output = MutexGuard<'a, T>;\n348. \n349.     #[inline]\n350.     fn poll_with_strategy<'x, S: event_listener_strategy::Strategy<'x>>(\n351.         self: Pin<&mut Self>,\n352.         strategy: &mut S,\n353.         context: &mut S::Context,\n354.     ) -> Poll<Self::Output> {\n355.         let mut this = self.project();\n356. \n357.         // This may seem weird, but the borrow checker complains otherwise.\n358.         if this.acquire_slow.is_none() {\n359.             match this.mutex.try_lock() {\n360. None => {\n362.                     this.acquire_slow.set(Some(AcquireSlow::new(this.mutex)));\n363.                 }\n364.             }\n365.         }\n366. \n367.         ready!(this\n368.             .acquire_slow\n369.             .as_pin_mut()\n370.             .unwrap()\n371.             .poll_with_strategy(strategy, context));\n372. Unpolled { mutex: Option<Arc<Mutex<T>>> },\n388. \n389.         /// We are acquiring the mutex through the slow path.\n390.         AcquireSlow {\n391.             #[pin]\n392.             inner: AcquireSlow<Arc<Mutex<T>>, T>\n393.         },\n394.     }\n395. }\n396. \n397. unsafe impl<T: Send + ?Sized> Send for LockArc<T> {}\n398. unsafe impl<T: Sync + ?Sized> Sync for LockArc<T> {}\n399. \n400. impl<T: ?Sized> fmt::Debug for LockArcInnards<T> {\n401.     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n402.         f.write_str(\"LockArc { .. }\")\n403.     }\n404. }\n405. \n406. impl<T: ?Sized> EventListenerFuture for LockArcInnards<T> {\n407.     type Output = MutexGuardArc<T>;\n408. \n409.     fn poll_with_strategy<'a, S: event_listener_strategy::Strategy<'a>>(\n410.         mut self: Pin<&mut Self>,\n411.         strategy: &mut S,\n412.         context: &mut S::Context,\n413.     ) -> Poll<Self::Output> {\n414.         // Set the inner future if needed.\n415.         if let LockArcInnardsProj::Unpolled { mutex } = self.as_mut().project() {\n416.             let mutex = mutex.take().expect(\"mutex taken more than once\");\n417. \n418.             // Try the fast path before trying to register slowly.\n419.             if let Some(guard) = mutex.try_lock_arc() {\n420.                 return Poll::Ready(guard);\n421.             }\n422. \n423.             // Set the inner future to the slow acquire path.\n424.             self.as_mut().set(LockArcInnards::AcquireSlow {\n425.                 inner: AcquireSlow::new(mutex),\n426.             });\n427.         }\n428. \n429.         // Poll the inner future.\n430.         let value = match self.project() {\n431.             LockArcInnardsProj::AcquireSlow { inner } => {\n432.                 ready!(inner.poll_with_strategy(strategy, context))\n433.             }\n434.             _ => unreachable!(),\n435.         };\n436. \n437.         Poll::Ready(MutexGuardArc(value))\n438.     }\n439. }\n440. \n441. pin_project_lite::pin_project! {\n442.     /// Future for acquiring the mutex slowly.\n443.     struct AcquireSlow<B: Borrow<Mutex<T>>, T: ?Sized> {\n444.         // Reference to the mutex.\n445.         mutex: Option<B>,\n446. \n447.         // The event listener waiting on the mutex.\n448.         #[pin]\n449.         listener: EventListener,\n450. \n451.         // The point at which the mutex lock was started.\n452.         start: Start,\n453. \n454.         // This lock operation is starving.\n455.         starved: bool,\n456. \n457.         // Capture the `T` lifetime.\n458.         #[pin]\n459.         _marker: PhantomData<T>,\n460.     }\n461. \n462.     impl<T: ?Sized, B: Borrow<Mutex<T>>> PinnedDrop for AcquireSlow<B, T> {\n463.         fn drop(this: Pin<&mut Self>) {\n464.             // Make sure the starvation counter is decremented.\n465.             this.take_mutex();\n466.         }\n467.     }\n468. }\n469. \n470. /// `pin_project_lite` doesn't support `#[cfg]` yet, so we have to do this manually.\n471. struct Start {\n472.     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n473.     start: Option<Instant>,\n474. }\n475. \n476. impl<T: ?Sized, B: Borrow<Mutex<T>>> AcquireSlow<B, T> {\n477.     /// Create a new `AcquireSlow` future.\n478.     #[cold]\n479.     fn new(mutex: B) -> Self {\n480.         // Create a new instance of the listener.\n481.         let listener = {\n482.             let mutex = Borrow::<Mutex<T>>::borrow(&mutex);\n483.             EventListener::new(&mutex.lock_ops)\n484.         };\n485. \n486.         AcquireSlow {\n487.             mutex: Some(mutex),\n488.             listener,\n489.             start: Start {\n490.                 #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n491.                 start: None,\n492.             },\n493.             starved: false,\n494.             _marker: PhantomData,\n495.         }\n496.     }\n497. \n498.     /// Take the mutex reference out, decrementing the counter if necessary.\n499.     fn take_mutex(self: Pin<&mut Self>) -> Option<B> {\n500.         let this = self.project();\n501.         let mutex = this.mutex.take();\n502. \n503.         if *this.starved {\n504.             if let Some(mutex) = mutex.as_ref() {\n505.                 // Decrement this counter before we exit.\n506.                 mutex.borrow().state.fetch_sub(2, Ordering::Release);\n507.             }\n508.         }\n509. \n510.         mutex\n511.     }\n512. }\n513. \n514. impl<T: ?Sized, B: Unpin + Borrow<Mutex<T>>> EventListenerFuture for AcquireSlow<B, T> {\n515.     type Output = B;\n516. \n517.     #[cold]\n518.     fn poll_with_strategy<'a, S: event_listener_strategy::Strategy<'a>>(\n519.         mut self: Pin<&mut Self>,\n520.         strategy: &mut S,\n521.         context: &mut S::Context,\n522.     ) -> Poll<Self::Output> {\n523.         let mut this = self.as_mut().project();\n524.         #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n525.         let start = *this.start.start.get_or_insert_with(Instant::now);\n526.         let mutex = Borrow::<Mutex<T>>::borrow(\n527.             this.mutex.as_ref().expect(\"future polled after completion\"),\n528.         );\n529. \n530.         // Only use this hot loop if we aren't currently starved.\n531.         if !*this.starved {\n532.             loop {\n533.                 // Start listening for events.\n534.                 if !this.listener.is_listening() {\n535.                     this.listener.as_mut().listen();\n536. \n537.                     // Try locking if nobody is being starved.\n538.                     match mutex\n539.                         .state\n540.                         .compare_exchange(0, 1, Ordering::Acquire, Ordering::Acquire)\n541.                         .unwrap_or_else(|x| x)\n542.                     {\n543.                         // Lock acquired!\n544.                         0 => return Poll::Ready(self.take_mutex().unwrap()),\n545. \n546.                         // Lock is held and nobody is starved.\n547.                         1 => {}\n548. \n549.                         // Somebody is starved.\n550.                         _ => break,\n551.                     }\n552.                 } else {\n553.                     ready!(strategy.poll(this.listener.as_mut(), context));\n554. \n555.                     // Try locking if nobody is being starved.\n556.                     match mutex\n557.                         .state\n558.                         .compare_exchange(0, 1, Ordering::Acquire, Ordering::Acquire)\n559.                         .unwrap_or_else(|x| x)\n560.                     {\n561.                         // Lock acquired!\n562.                         0 => return Poll::Ready(self.take_mutex().unwrap()),\n563. \n564.                         // Lock is held and nobody is starved.\n565.                         1 => {}\n566. \n567.                         // Somebody is starved.\n568.                         _ => {\n569.                             // Notify the first listener in line because we probably received a\n570.                             // notification that was meant for a starved task.\n571.                             mutex.lock_ops.notify(1);\n572.                             break;\n573.                         }\n574.                     }\n575. \n576.                     // If waiting for too long, fall back to a fairer locking strategy that will prevent\n577.                     // newer lock operations from starving us forever.\n578.                     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n579.                     if start.elapsed() > Duration::from_micros(500) {\n580.                         break;\n581.                     }\n582.                 }\n583.             }\n584. \n585.             // Increment the number of starved lock operations.\n586.             if mutex.state.fetch_add(2, Ordering::Release) > usize::MAX / 2 {\n587.                 // In case of potential overflow, abort.\n588.                 crate::abort();\n589.             }\n590. \n591.             // Indicate that we are now starving and will use a fairer locking strategy.\n592.             *this.starved = true;\n593.         }\n594. \n595.         // Fairer locking loop.\n596.         loop {\n597.             if !this.listener.is_listening() {\n598.                 // Start listening for events.\n599.                 this.listener.as_mut().listen();\n600. \n601.                 // Try locking if nobody else is being starved.\n602.                 match mutex\n603.                     .state\n604.                     .compare_exchange(2, 2 | 1, Ordering::Acquire, Ordering::Acquire)\n605.                     .unwrap_or_else(|x| x)\n606.                 {\n607.                     // Lock acquired!\n608.                     2 => return Poll::Ready(self.take_mutex().unwrap()),\n609. \n610.                     // Lock is held by someone.\n611.                     s if s % 2 == 1 => {}\n612. \n613.                     // Lock is available.\n614.                     _ => {\n615.                         // Be fair: notify the first listener and then go wait in line.\n616.                         mutex.lock_ops.notify(1);\n617.                     }\n618.                 }\n619.             } else {\n620.                 // Wait for a notification.\n621.                 ready!(strategy.poll(this.listener.as_mut(), context));\n622. \n623.                 // Try acquiring the lock without waiting for others.\n624.                 if mutex.state.fetch_or(1, Ordering::Acquire) % 2 == 0 {\n625.                     return Poll::Ready(self.take_mutex().unwrap());\n626.                 }\n627.             }\n628.         }\n629.     }\n630. }\n631. \n632. /// Raw, unsafe reader-writer locking implementation,\n2. //! doesn't depend on the data protected by the lock.\n3. //! [`RwLock`](super::RwLock) is implemented in terms of this.\n4. //!\n5. //! Splitting the implementation this way allows instantiating\n6. //! the locking code only once, and also lets us make\n7. //! [`RwLockReadGuard`](super::RwLockReadGuard) covariant in `T`.\n8. \n9. use core::mem::forget;\n10. use core::pin::Pin;\n11. use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering};\n12. use core::task::Poll;\n13. \n14. use event_listener::{Event, EventListener};\n15. use event_listener_strategy::{EventListenerFuture, Strategy};\n16. \n17. use crate::futures::Lock;\n18. use crate::Mutex;\n19. \n20. const WRITER_BIT: usize = 1;\n21. const ONE_READER: usize = 2;\n22. \n23. /// A \"raw\" RwLock that doesn't hold any data.\n24. pub(super) struct RawRwLock {\n25.     /// Acquired by the writer.\n26.     mutex: Mutex<()>,\n27. \n28.     /// Event triggered when the last reader is dropped.\n29.     no_readers: Event,\n30. \n31.     /// Event triggered when the writer is dropped.\n32.     no_writer: Event,\n33. \n34.     /// Current state of the lock.\n35.     ///\n36.     /// The least significant bit (`WRITER_BIT`) is set to 1 when a writer is holding the lock or\n37.     /// trying to acquire it.\n38.     ///\n39.     /// The upper bits contain the number of currently active readers. Each active reader\n40.     /// increments the state by `ONE_READER`.\n41.     state: AtomicUsize,\n42. }\n43. \n44. impl RawRwLock {\n45.     #[inline]\n46.     pub(super) const fn new() -> Self {\n47.         RawRwLock {\n48.             mutex: Mutex::new(()),\n49.             no_readers: Event::new(),\n50.             no_writer: Event::new(),\n51.             state: AtomicUsize::new(0),\n52.         }\n53.     }\n54. \n55.     /// Returns `true` iff a read lock was successfully acquired.\n56.     pub(super) fn try_read(&self) -> bool {\n57.         let mut state = self.state.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n58. \n59.         loop {\n60.             // If there's a writer holding the lock or attempting to acquire it, we cannot acquire\n61.             // a read lock here.\n62.             if state & WRITER_BIT != 0 {\n63.                 return false;\n64.             }\n65. \n66.             // Make sure the number of readers doesn't overflow.\n67.             if state > core::isize::MAX as usize {\n68.                 crate::abort();\n69.             }\n70. \n71.             // Increment the number of readers.\n72.             match self.state.compare_exchange(\n73.                 state,\n74.                 state + ONE_READER,\n75.                 Ordering::AcqRel,\n76.                 Ordering::Acquire,\n77.             ) {\n78.                 Ok(_) => return true,\n79.                 Err(s) => state = s,\n80.             }\n81.         }\n82.     }\n83. \n84.     #[inline]\n85.     pub(super) fn read(&self) -> RawRead<'_> {\n86.         RawRead {\n87.             lock: self,\n88.             state: self.state.load(Ordering::Acquire),\n89.             listener: EventListener::new(&self.no_writer),\n90.         }\n91.     }\n92. \n93.     /// Returns `true` iff an upgradable read lock was successfully acquired.\n94. \n95.     pub(super) fn try_upgradable_read(&self) -> bool {\n96.         // First try grabbing the mutex.\n97.         let lock = if let Some(lock) = self.mutex.try_lock() {\n98.             lock\n99.         } else {\n100.             return false;\n101.         };\n102. \n103.         forget(lock);\n104. \n105.         let mut state = self.state.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n106. \n107.         // Make sure the number of readers doesn't overflow.\n108.         if state > core::isize::MAX as usize {\n109.             crate::abort();\n110.         }\n111. \n112.         // Increment the number of readers.\n113.         loop {\n114.             match self.state.compare_exchange(\n115.                 state,\n116.                 state + ONE_READER,\n117.                 Ordering::AcqRel,\n118.                 Ordering::Acquire,\n119.             ) {\n120.                 Ok(_) => return true,\n121.                 Err(s) => state = s,\n122.             }\n123.         }\n124.     }\n125. \n126.     #[inline]\n127. \n128.     pub(super) fn upgradable_read(&self) -> RawUpgradableRead<'_> {\n129. Safety\n239.     ///\n240.     /// Caller must hold a read lock .\n241.     /// This will unlock that lock.\n242. \n243.     pub(super) unsafe fn read_unlock(&self) {\n244.         // Decrement the number of readers.\n245.         if self.state.fetch_sub(ONE_READER, Ordering::SeqCst) & !WRITER_BIT == ONE_READER {\n246.             // If this was the last reader, trigger the \"no readers\" event.\n247.             self.no_readers.notify(1);\n248.         }\n249.     }\n250. \n251.     /// # Safety\n252.     ///\n253.     /// Caller must hold an upgradable read lock.\n254.     /// This will unlock that lock.\n255. \n256.     pub(super) unsafe fn upgradable_read_unlock(&self) {\n257.         // Decrement the number of readers.\n258.         if self.state.fetch_sub(ONE_READER, Ordering::SeqCst) & !WRITER_BIT == ONE_READER {\n259.             // If this was the last reader, trigger the \"no readers\" event.\n260.             self.no_readers.notify(1);\n261.         }\n262. \n263.         // SAFETY: upgradable read guards acquire the writer mutex upon creation.\n264.         self.mutex.unlock_unchecked();\n265.     }\n266. \n267.     /// # Ordering::AcqRel,\n323.                     Ordering::Acquire,\n324.                 ) {\n325.                     Ok(_) => return Poll::Ready(()),\n326.                     Err(s) => *this.state = s,\n327.                 }\n328.             } else {\n329.                 // Start listening for \"no writer\" events.\n330.                 let load_ordering = if !this.listener.is_listening() {\n331.                     this.listener.as_mut().listen();\n332. \n333.                     // Make sure there really is no writer.\n334.                     Ordering::SeqCst\n335.                 } else {\n336.                     // Wait for the writer to finish.\n337.                     ready!(strategy.poll(this.listener.as_mut(), cx));\n338. \n339.                     // Notify the next reader waiting in list.\n340.                     this.lock.no_writer.notify(1);\n341. \n342.                     // Check the state again.\n343.                     Ordering::Acquire\n344.                 };\n345. \n346.                 // Reload the state.\n347.                 *this.state = this.lock.state.load(load_ordering);\n348.             }\n349.         }\n350.     }\n351. }\n352. \n353. pin_project_lite::pin_project! {\n354.     /// The future returned by [`RawRwLock::upgradable_read`].\n355.     pub(super) struct RawUpgradableRead<'a> {\n356.         // The lock that is being acquired.\n357.         pub(super) lock: &'a RawRwLock,\n358. \n359.         // The mutex we are trying to acquire.\n360.         #[pin]\n361.         acquire: Lock<'a, ()>,\n362.     }\n363. }\n364. \n365. impl<'a> EventListenerFuture for RawUpgradableRead<'a> {\n366.     type Output = ();\n367. \n368.     fn poll_with_strategy<'x, S: Strategy<'x>>(\n369.         self: Pin<&mut Self>,\n370.         strategy: &mut S,\n371.         cx: &mut S::Context,\n372.     ) -> Poll<()> {\n373.         let this = self.project();\n374. \n375.         // Acquire the mutex.\n376.         let mutex_guard = ready!(this.acquire.poll_with_strategy(strategy, cx));\n377.         forget(mutex_guard);\n378. \n379.         // Load the current state.\n380.         let mut state = this.lock.state.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n381. \n382.         // Make sure the number of readers doesn't overflow.\n383.         if state > core::isize::MAX as usize {\n384.             crate::abort();\n385.         }\n386. \n387.         // Increment the number of readers.\n388.         loop {\n389.             match this.lock.state.compare_exchange(\n390.                 state,\n391.                 state + ONE_READER,\n392. WaitingReaders,\n443. \n444.         // The future has completed.\n445.         Acquired,\n446.     }\n447. }\n448. \n449. impl<'a> EventListenerFuture for RawWrite<'a> {\n450.     type Output = ();\n451. \n452.     fn poll_with_strategy<'x, S: Strategy<'x>>(\n453.         self: Pin<&mut Self>,\n454.         strategy: &mut S,\n455.         cx: &mut S::Context,\n456.     ) -> Poll<()> {\n457.         let mut this = self.project();\n458. \n459.         loop {\n460.             match this.state.as_mut().project() {\n461.                 WriteStateProj::Acquiring { lock } => {\n462.                     // First grab the mutex.\n463.                     let mutex_guard = ready!(lock.poll_with_strategy(strategy, cx));\n464.                     forget(mutex_guard);\n465. \n466.                     // Set `WRITER_BIT` and create a guard that unsets it in case this future is canceled.\n467.                     let new_state = this.lock.state.fetch_or(WRITER_BIT, Ordering::SeqCst);\n468. \n469.                     // If we just acquired the lock, return.\n470.                     if new_state == WRITER_BIT {\n471.                         this.state.as_mut().set(WriteState::Acquired);\n472.                         return Poll::Ready(());\n473.                     }\n474. \n475.                     // Start waiting for the readers to finish.\n476.                     this.no_readers.as_mut().listen();\n477.                     this.state.as_mut().set(WriteState::WaitingReaders);\n478.                 }\n479. \n480.                 WriteStateProj::WaitingReaders => {\n481.                     let load_ordering = if this.no_readers.is_listening() {\n482.                         Ordering::Acquire\n483.                     } else {\n484.                         Ordering::SeqCst\n485.                     };\n486. \n487.                     // Check the state again.\n488.                     if this.lock.state.load(load_ordering) == WRITER_BIT {\n489.                         // We are the only ones holding the lock, return `Ready`.\n490.                         this.state.as_mut().set(WriteState::Acquired);\n491.                         return Poll::Ready(());\n492.                     }\n493. \n494.                     // Wait for the readers to finish.\n495.                     if !this.no_readers.is_listening() {\n496.                         // Register a listener.\n497.                         this.no_readers.as_mut().listen();\n498.                     } else {\n499.                         // Wait for the readers to finish.\n500.                         ready!(strategy.poll(this.no_readers.as_mut(), cx));\n501.                     };\n502.                 }\n503.                 WriteStateProj::Acquired => panic!(\"Write lock already acquired\"),\n504.             }\n505.         }\n506.     }\n507. }\n508. \n509. pin_project_lite::pin_project! {\n510.     /// The future returned by [`RawRwLock::upgrade`].\n511. \n512.     pub(super) struct RawUpgrade<'a> {\n513.         lock: Option<&'a RawRwLock>,\n514. \n515.         // The event listener we are waiting on.\n516.         #[pin]\n517.         listener: EventListener,\n518.     }\n519. \n520.     impl PinnedDrop for RawUpgrade<'_> {\n521.         fn drop(this: Pin<&mut Self>) {\n522.             let this = this.project();\n523.             if let Some(lock) = this.lock {\n524.                 // SAFETY: we are dropping the future that would give us a write lock,\n525.                 // so we don't need said lock anymore.\n526.                 unsafe {\n527.                     lock.write_unlock();\n528.                 }\n529.             }\n530.         }\n531.     }\n532. }\n533. \n534. impl<'a> EventListenerFuture for RawUpgrade<'a> {\n535.     type Output = &'a RawRwLock;\n536. \n537.     fn poll_with_strategy<'x, S: Strategy<'x>>(\n538.         self: Pin<&mut Self>,\n539.         strategy: &mut S,\n540.         cx: &mut S::Context,\n541.     ) -> Poll<&'a RawRwLock> {\n542.         let mut this = self.project();\n543.         let lock = this.lock.expect(\"cannot poll future after completion\");\n544. \n545.         // If there are readers, we need to wait for them to finish.\n546.         loop {\n547.             let load_ordering = if this.listener.is_listening() {\n548. Ordering::SeqCst\n551.             };\n552. \n553.             // See if the number of readers is zero.\n554.             let state = lock.state.load(load_ordering);\n555.             if state == WRITER_BIT {\n556.                 break;\n557.             }\n558. \n559.             // If there are readers, wait for them to finish.\n560.             if !this.listener.is_listening() {\n561.                 // Start listening for \"no readers\" events.\n562.                 this.listener.as_mut().listen();\n563.             } else {\n564.                 // Wait for the readers to finish.\n565.                 ready!(strategy.poll(this.listener.as_mut(), cx));\n566.             };\n567.         }\n568. \n569.         // We are done.\n570. It is intended to be\n105.     /// used in a way that a semaphore can be used in both asynchronous and synchronous contexts.\n106.     /// Calling this method in an asynchronous context may result in a deadlock.\n107.     ///\n108.     /// # Examples\n109.     ///\n110.     /// ```\n111.     /// use async_lock::Semaphore;\n112.     ///\n113.     /// let s = Semaphore::new(2);\n114.     /// let guard = s.acquire_blocking();\n115.     /// ```\n116.     #[cfg(all(feature = \"std\", not(target_family = \"wasm\")))]\n117.     #[inline]\n118.     pub fn acquire_blocking(&self) -> SemaphoreGuard<'_> {\n119.         self.acquire().wait()\n120.     }\n121. \n122.     /// Attempts to get an owned permit for a concurrent operation.\n123.     ///\n124.     /// If the permit could not be acquired at this time, then [`None`] is returned. Err(c) => count = c,\n157.             }\n158.         }\n159.     }\n160. \n161.     /// Waits for an owned permit for a concurrent operation.\n162.     ///\n163.     /// Returns a guard that releases the permit when dropped.\n164.     ///\n165.     /// # Examples\n166.     ///\n167.     /// ```\n168.     /// # futures_lite::future::block_on(async {\n169.     /// use async_lock::Semaphore;\n170.     /// use std::sync::Arc;\n171.     ///\n172.     /// let s = Arc::new(Semaphore::new(2));\n173.     /// let guard = s.acquire_arc().await;\n174.     /// # });\n175.     /// ```\n176.     pub fn acquire_arc(self: &Arc<Self>) -> AcquireArc {\n177.
